And set this secret white and red bodhicitta
As the essence of drops and syllables.
With the mudrā of the vajra dance
Of the blazing karma vajra,
Draw out this line of bodhicitta!
Draw out this secret vajra activity!
Perform this secret vajra activity!
Through the application of the secret method
Called oṃ candradhara hrī,
With the drop of the blazing supreme substance,
Make the drop of the secret method
In the palaces of the five places.
When that application of the cord is complete,
Lay out this vajra line of the Middle Way,
Free from the faults of deviation, obscuration, eternalism and nihilism,
Neither too large nor too small, without crookedness.

In the palace of the dharmadhātu,
On the open maṇḍala beyond reference,
As the lines of the five wisdoms radiate,
There is the assembly of the mudrās of great bliss and liberation.
In the palace of the space of the all-ground,
On the maṇḍala of afflicted thoughts,
As the lines of the light rays of samādhi radiate,
There is the assembly of the blazing vajra mudrās.
In the secret palace of the expanse of space,
From the maṇḍala of the crescent moon and lotus,
As the lines of bodhicitta radiate,
There is the assembly of radiant wisdom beings.
In the palace of auspicious solitude,
On the maṇḍala that supports the signs,
As the lines of the vajra cord radiate,
The miraculous images, the mudrā deities, blaze.
When the marvelous vajra line is stretched out
In the secret maṇḍala of the trichiliocosm,
For one who abides by the secret pledge,
Against bhūtas and vile beings,
From the long hūṃ of vajra power
A storm of vajra weapons whirls.
The directions, intermediate directions, above and below are filled with wrathful ones.
Seizing the blazing secret boundary,
Cut off the disruptions of the outsiders.
The assembly of blazing vajra weapons,
Fierce in burning and crushing,
Is to strike here with the vajra tongue.

In the vajra palace of the sun,
The yogin should lay down the line of light of the vajra śrīvatsa,
Having the nature of mirror-like wisdom.
Likewise, in the blazing jeweled palace,
And in the immeasurable sky palaces
Of the lotus, the moon and the wheel,
The yogin should lay down the line of light of the śrīvatsa of each family,
Having the nature of the four wisdoms.
In vajra space turn the wheel.
With the four pairs of skillful means and wisdom
Each stationed at its own hand,
At the heart of the center of space,
Snap the four cords crosswise.
Then the vajra ground of stability,
The ninth palace, the central one,
And, with the great line of the vajra cord,
All the places where the mudrā deities reside

Should be made even, neither too large nor too small.
Here there are seven vajra enclosures;
Up to the sixth stage of the elements,
Six stacked wheels blaze at the core.
At the cut corners and between the spokes, fire mountains radiate.
Three rings of enclosure form garlands of blaze,
Adorned by four intermediate courtyards and an outer courtyard,
And nine doors rise in clear tiers toward the east.
In each place of that palace,
The forms of the mudrā deities appear thus:
Out to the very last enclosure,
The sixty-four fathers and mothers,
Their measureless miracles and samayas,
Their play, the Terrifier and the Great Terrifier,
Are to be drawn on kacali and the like.
Likewise the four devouring fathers and mothers,
The deities of activity, the Herukas,
Are to be placed on the spokes of the last wheel.
The mudrā deities of the sacred places and lands
Are to be arranged outside and inside the cut corners.
The fathers and mothers of the ten great Hūṃ deities,
Very beautiful, are on the first;
On that same one, the eight mothers
And the eighteen great kiṅkaras are to be set out in full.
In the second courtyard, the eight ḍākinīs,
And likewise the eight swift-goers.
In the third courtyard, draw the eight phramen
And likewise the eight blazing goddesses.
In the fourth courtyard, the eight cup-bearers,
And likewise the eight rākṣasīs bearing troughs;
They are to be drawn beautiful in every way.
In the fifth courtyard, the last,
Arrange in full the vajra attendants, the maidservants,
The messengers and the rest.
As for setting them out in the proper way:
The ones above are drawn from the materials,
On kacali and the like,
According to what one can afford.
All the articles of offering
And the marvelous supreme substances
Are to be arranged in the vajra secret sequence.
Having opened the vajra secret door
And completed the whole maṇḍala of samādhi,
Bring it down from its natural domain
And complete it there in the maṇḍala of accomplishment.

Here too, by a distinction of meaning,
Raising them to the ground of the great vidyādharas:
On the special seat of the east,
A wheel borne up by vajra lions,
With moon and sun to the right and left
Serving as footrests, the maṇḍala
Where the Vajra King, father and mother, sits.
On the special seat of the south,
A wheel borne up by vajra elephants,
With lotuses to the right and left serving as footrests,
Where the Vajra Victor, father and mother, sits.
On the special seat of the north,
A crossed vajra borne up by lotus and vajra,
Where Vajrakarma, father and mother, sits.
In the west, or in a direction or another intermediate direction,
Where the bases have become one,
On an unbroken maṇḍala
Filled and linked with vajras,
A lotus blazes greatly on one side,
Wreathed in the light of blazing colors.
By the distinction of greater or lesser capacity,
For some, whether fortunate or not,
The sign of the seat
Is a full moon.
And again, according to greater or lesser capacity,
Some are marked with a three-pointed vajra,
Some are marked with a half vajra,
Some are to be marked with a crescent moon not yet full,
Its opening shown facing inward.
Some are marked with a piercing point,
Some are marked with stacked lotuses,
And for some there is no mark.
Some do not sit upon that seat;
The long human substance of a person
Is arranged as a means for each one's comfort.
To make it comfortable in every way,
It is known as the pipar cushion,
Arranged so that it fully supports the body in comfort.

In the marvelous maṇḍala of accomplishment,
Perform the activities of entering,
Of requesting accomplishment,
And of empowerment.
For accomplishing what is desired,
The substances such as torma
Are divided into three portions:
One for the supreme activity of the fire offering,
One as a treasure of the enjoyments of yoga,
And one to nourish the retinue and assembly of ḍākinīs.
At the site, with auspicious verses and the like,
When the accomplishment is complete, bring it to a close.

In the very expanse of the supreme secret charnel ground,
The nine stacked maṇḍalas are the seal of glory;
The inner space of the nine wisdoms is clear;
The lines and cords of fire-mountain light are laid down.
The ground where the great gathering of the wheel is accomplished,
The abode of those with supreme yogic power:
That root maṇḍala of accomplishment
Is the sūtra of the supreme secret charnel ground.

This was the twelfth chapter, on the method of accomplishment of the Great Gathering Assembly, from the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness.

Then the sublime Sattvavajra declared: "Assembly of the Great Assembly, if all vidyādharas wish to attain accomplishment with certainty, they should understand it thus:

Likewise, when all has been completed,
Distinguish these particulars of family,
And accomplish in each one's own family.
For the Vajra Holder, the śrīvatsa,
And the maṇḍala is a blazing vajra.
Likewise for the jewel, a jewel,
And for the red lotus, a center of light.
For activity, a crossed vajra blazing on all sides.
Likewise the jewel blazes as a wheel.
Set each family as the principal,
And the retinue likewise, unchanged;
Having arranged them and applied them well,
Accomplish completely the approach and the close approach,
The accomplishment and the great accomplishment.
As for the particulars of the power of samādhi:
Whatever one's own family may be,
On its many particular samādhis
Make the application of mind clear.
By that, whatever vidyādhara state is desired
And prayed for will certainly be accomplished."
Thus he spoke.

This was the thirteenth chapter, on accomplishing the vajra vidyādhara, from the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness.

Then again Sattvavajra said: "Assembly of the Great Assembly, all phenomena should be understood as without acceptance or rejection.

In the nature of the equality of the nature of reality,
There are no words that conceive of good and bad;
There is no division into self and other,
And no duality of pure and impure."
Thus he spoke.

Then many vidyādharas bearing human skulls, who were gathered in that retinue, rose in array from their rows of yoga, uttered the great sound alala ho, and said, "We wish to abide fully in what the Blessed One has declared, yet the Blessed One has not spoken of our samayas."

The Blessed One declared, "You bearers of human skulls, take courage and offer your samayas, each as it pleases you."

Then the vidyādhara of desire, the brahmin bearer of a human skull called Melody of Vajra Ash, said this: "Blessed One, our samaya is this:

Mother, sister, daughter,
All the women of the world
Of moving and unmoving beings, every one:
Whatever passion of desire one may enjoy with them,
If one understands correctly, one is not stained."
So he said.

Then the vidyādhara called Rakṣa, bearer of a human skull, said this:

"Blessed One, my samaya is this:
I delight in killing.
Having killed all sentient beings,
To eat the defilements, the impure dregs,
Human flesh and all the rest, everything,
Is the supreme samaya,
And power and splendor will increase.
That is my samaya."
So he said.

Then again the yakṣa vidyādhara called Yakṣa, bearer of a human skull, said this: "Blessed One, my samaya is this:

Though one robs all the lords of men
Of everything they desire,
And steals gold and the like and enjoys it,
If one understands correctly, one is not stained,
And it becomes the supreme fulfillment of desire.
That is my samaya."
So he said.

Then again the tīrthika vidyādhara called Kāśyapa of the Matted Locks said this: "Blessed One, my samaya is this:

Having cut off human heads,
One should examine with awareness
And come to know their differences, good and bad,
And make use of those that bear the good signs:
White, with a goat's-hair crown and willow-like locks.
Whoever always wears these
Will accomplish every desire
And be able to vanquish every opponent.
Like a wish-fulfilling jewel,
Their virtues arise from this.
That is my samaya."
So he said.

Then again the brahmins who delight in flesh and the piśācī vidyādharas said this: "Blessed One, our samaya is this:

The flesh of cattle, dogs and horses,
Human flesh, elephant flesh and the rest:
All flesh is alike, neither pure nor defiled.
If one eats it, various virtues are accomplished.
That is our samaya."
So they said.

Then again the rākṣasī vidyādharas, the one called Blood Snatcher and the others, said this: "Blessed One, our samaya is this:

The warm blood
Of all living beings who have been slain,
If one drinks it, splendor will blaze.
That is our samaya."
So they said.

The goddesses, the nāginīs, the low-caste women and all the hosts of women said this: "Our samaya is this:

Dance and great songs
Are supreme among all things to rely on.
With the graceful ways and great movements of dance,
With the sweetness of song and the proclaiming of the meaning of its words,
We steal away all the faculties of the mind.
Engaging in sexual union as the means of reliance:
This is the samaya of delight;
It is our samaya."
So they said.

Then all those vidyādharas said this: "Blessed One, our common samaya is this:

Whatever white or black action one may do,
There is no difference or distinction
Of higher, middling or lower
In the result of its karmic maturation.
Likewise, in this there is no dispute
About happiness and suffering, or virtue and nonvirtue.
It cannot be cut with a sharp weapon.
Why? Because it is empty in seven ways,
For it is unobstructed.
Life, too, cannot be cut off or killed.
Why? Because this substance perishes anyway.
All actions of great evil,
Whether done by oneself or caused to be done by another,
If one gives gifts to beings
As many as the sands of the Ganges,
And likewise kills all beings
As many as the sands of the Ganges,
For one who understands correctly, the two are no different,
Just as dust and water
Do not cling to the sky.
Whoever has shame and scruple,
By holding to doubting thoughts,
Will fall into the great hell.
Those beings who torment their bodies
With practices of abstaining from passion and the like
Are of small understanding."
So they said.

Then the Blessed One Sattvavajra said this:
"How wonderful! Host of skull-bearers,
These words that you have spoken
Are words bound up in knots;
You will not become free of the burden.
Saying 'Correct, correct!'
Is the knot of correctness itself.
If you do not untie the knot,
You will not be released."

"Those who engage in the acts of passion,
If they engage in them under the power of passion,
Will migrate into its city
And become beings of that realm.
As for the great acts that transform perception,
If the mind slips under the power of hatred,
They are a mountain of evil
That makes one fall as a vidyādhara of slaughtering butchers.
Since the Victors are pure,
When the face of the Sugata, the Victor,
Is served with impurities
Of every various kind,
Then, through the maturation of that karma,
Seized by an incurable disease,
Will one not sink into the mire?
Food such as flesh and blood
Is a great food of rank stench;
On the bodies of those who eat it,
Bhūtas and gandharvas all gather
And draw out the stench, the taste and the essence.
If by this splendor and radiance fade,
Why then should one eat these things?
While the mind itself is a precious jewel,
To make use of another's jewel
Is a practice of taking the wrong by the wrong;
These are tight knots.
To say there is no difference between white and black actions
And to conduct everything in a mixed way
Is the perverse action of those without lineage or with lineage cut off;
Its result becomes a perverse realm.
In the acts meant to please the Sugatas,
If limbs and words are not pure,
Through that cause, the act of wrongdoing,
At all times
One's faculties and limbs will be incomplete
And the heart of mindfulness will decline.
If the yogin who holds awareness,
Meditating on and reciting the Sugata, the Victor,
Takes it there as a mere sign,
Like the display of a miraculous wrathful manner,
He is a vidyādhara of the rākṣasas of karma.
If all ornaments and attire
Are conceived as mere signs,
They become a great stake of grasping
That conceives of and clings to everything.
If all the causal syllables,
Such as a and hūṃ,
Are conceived there as mere signs,
They are a great obstacle of conceptual thought
That makes one fall among the venomous snakes of the sandalwood tree and the like.
If the five great substances such as blood
Are conceived as real things in themselves,
Then whoever understands them there conceptually
Takes on a body of the kind that bhūtas have, stinking of blood,
In the fierce guise of the seven mothers:
Rākṣasīs and ugly piśācīs,
They wander in the land of Laṅkā.
Those whose ransom rites rest on signs, with effigy heads,
Fixating on 'Here it is strong, here it is seized,'
Will become the class of great predators that haunt the toll gates,
Grasping and conceiving in those places.
If the host of maidservants mighty in activity
Are conceived as being just so,
One will become one of the host of great yakṣiṇīs
Who harm the lives of others.
Therefore this great knot
Is a load carried as a burden;
Through corruption, the eye of the view is blind,
And that precious jewel of the mind
Will be lost in the realms of eternalism and nihilism.
Abandon such great faults!"

When he had declared this, the hosts of skull-bearers were seized by the perception of realization, and weariness with their own conduct arose in them. Then they said this to the Blessed One: "How wonderful, Blessed One! Since our samayas have turned into perverse austerities, and we wish to abide in true austerity: all phenomena are like illusions and can become anything at all. The door of the great secret mantra is like the fire mass at the end of the eon. Please explain it according to each of our conceptions and according to our inclinations."

The Blessed One laughed with great delight and said, "Sons of good family, it is good that you ask in this way. Listen well! Without forbidding your conduct, each of your intentions shall be satisfied as it pleases you; the inconceivable conduct of the world, bound to signs, shall be made into the great austerity of secret mantra. Without restraining your own conduct, act with your three doors at ease."

Then Sattvavajra again said this:
"Oh! Host of skull-bearers,
Women, all women without exception,
Are the support of generation
And also the field where the fruit ripens.
They are the place of liberating and drawing forth,
And the object of the joy of wisdom.

In this three minds abide:
The mind of the nature of reality, pure from the very beginning;
The mind of awakening, united with space;
And liberation through compassion. Joining these three,
The secret nature of reality pervades like the rising sun.
Liberation through compassion is joined by three:
By samādhi, by blessing, and by wisdom,
One liberates into space, the charnel ground of the nature of reality.

From the supreme cause of liberation on down,
The great crystal, the tsara, and the rest,
In these plant the seeds of the six causes,
And destroy them as they are, in their primordial nature.
The five syllables beyond all distinction,
Set in their places and radiating, fill the limitless.
Having emptied and exhausted everything,
They shine clearly as the five gathered syllables.
From them the hand emblems, the five wisdoms,
Dissolve into the state of non-abiding wisdom.
That which has ripened into an aggregate of obscuration
Should be joined, by the fierce host of vajra play,
With dance steps in the manner of drawing forth.
With the khaṭvāṅga and the rest, together with mantras,
Strike it down and open the door of ignorance.
Draw the consciousness out of the box of breath,
Gather it into the wind box of the Vajra King,
And for the one with vajra breath
Make hūṃ and blend it into that rolled pellet.
In the maṇḍala of the secret mind
Dissolve it as light a span in size.
Kindle it with yuti ahaṃ;
If it is clear, it shines together with that.
Blend it with vajra life.
With vajra wind kindle the breath,
And from the heart join it to the path of the wind box.
In the celestial palace of the Dharma, space,
Upon a sun it shines as hūṃ.
The wisdom being, a span in size,
Shines within the amulet box of sun and moon.
The a as well, propelled and hurled by phaṭ,
Radiates into the heart of Vajrasattva.
As it shines as the wisdom being,
Free of grasping at appearances as real things,
While moving, stirring, leaping and quivering do not cease,
Confer the vajra name empowerment,
And give the deity of the family and its heart mantra.
Singing the vajra song of longing,
Make the aspiration of the place of the pith instruction.

Having cut it cleanly into four parts,
Apply medicine and search through the remains.
Gather those aggregates of saṃsāra,
And purify them with vajra steps and song.
Dedicate them to each of the sacred places,
And dissolve the aggregates into offering substances.
Perform the vajra rites of bra.
By this come pleasing and fulfilling,
And the vajra activities are accomplished.

The yogin, great lord of activity,
From the lofty, pure snowy recess of wisdom,
With the claws of awareness, sharp and rending,
Destroys the elephant of pride and arrogance.
You lion cubs, yogins of full-grown prowess,
By training in this great vajra prowess,
The support to which the supreme deities of yoga come,
The jewel from which accomplishments arise,
The great means by which power and strength are born,
By this supreme empowerment through which blessings descend,
From the accomplishment of the Victors on down,
All the miracles of the ḍākinīs,
And the classes of savage, haughty spirits,
Since all of them will surely come under your power,
What need is there to speak of other bhūtas?

On the great dry island of saṃsāra,
The sentient beings circling through the six classes,
Their consciousness and life passed through the six streams,
Since they are slain by the weapon of the three times,
From the corpse island of those who died in the extremes of permanence and annihilation,
Taking up the great rotting corpse of delusion,
With wisdom, the great medicine of amṛta,
Taking up the corpses from the extremes of permanence and annihilation,
In order that they may shine as the mind of awakening,
One should seek the substances of the corpse.

Taking oneself to be the fierce wrathful host,
From within the blazing vajra eyes
Great lions of dark blue hūṃ
Are emanated into the realm of the corpse island of saṃsāra.
All the charnel grounds of liberation without exception
Should be seen as fields of the play of wisdom.
With the throwing techniques of the wrathful wrestlers,
In the fierce manner of great majesty,
Apply the sidelong gait of the vajra hero,
And apply the eighteen great methods as dance steps.
With the gait of the method of drawing forth,
Gaze warily and frolic;
Change the voice and shout he he;
Be fierce in fighting and contending.
Gaze in the stance of a female yak and act as one startled;
Look to the right and turn to the left;
Not trusting, look back again;
With angry eyes look all around to either side.
Run and go with the vajra gait;
Cry out the fierce sound ha ha ru lu;
Utter each its own symbolic call,
And leap and run in the manner of a kicking beast.

Having opened the great wish-fulfilling treasure,
That heap of jewels that grant all desires,
Having torn out by the root the ripening of saṃsāra,
Having shaken out for good the three great poisons,
With the gaping maw of fierce compassion
And the great host of blazing vajras,
Having reaped the harvest of accomplishments as well,
Without mistaking the direction the vajra face is turned,
Hold the secret place with two crystal daggers.
Upon the base of the one who is surely dead,
Kick it a little and turn it about.
By whichever way the face looks, examine good and bad.
That very good or ill outwardly
Is sure to be clearly shown within.
If signs of fault arise, the learned suppress them.

If one raises the matter by questioning,
To clear away the doubts about it:
Those who have entered conduct with real things
Put these conceptions about signs
To me, the lord of desire, bound by the command of Bhairava:
"Explain what was spoken,
And make clear the substances of signs.
Protector of the world, listen to me!
We shall ask about our conduct."

"Through the good or bad characteristics of the vessel,
Fault and good arise from this.
The signs of defect are sevenfold:
Lying face down, and moving from its seat;
The lineage not increasing, and cracking at the seams;
Hindrances arising, and obstructors rising up;
And bad signs appearing.
The learned never hold to these.
The causes of good are eleven:
Attaining the accomplishments of the three;
Going on the path of the vidyādharas and flying in the sky;
Bringing the three realms under power; the vajra eye;
Turning back enemy armies and overpowering them with splendor;
Power and blessing; increase of life;
Clear signs appearing;
The lineage of disciples increasing like water;
The intended aim being accomplished;
Swift buddhahood hereafter;
And attaining the three kinds of accomplishment:
It is accomplished in accordance with one's aspiration.
Moreover, the particulars are these:
If it turns out half, that is the share obtained;
With only a third, it is neutral."

Moreover, as for their individual marks and signs,
Which are to be examined and named:
The white one is the Dharma body free of elaboration;
The one with locks, complete in all, is endowed with enjoyment;
It is the stream of future emanation deeds.
The family of those of past, present and future
Who go to the grounds of yoga
Is the sign that the sugatas of the three times appear.
This amulet box of a treasure of jewels,
King of methods, wealth of glory,
Palace endowed with signs, complete as vessel and contents,
Magical face, a wish-fulfilling cow that yields all desires,
Supreme one that goes through the sky without legs,
Wondrous, with a palate that resounds,
Its marvelous secret self-sounds
Proclaim to all the vidyādharas of prowess:
"Since I am the marvelous substance of practice
Of the supreme being of prowess,
Whoever wants whatever accomplishment,
Take it now, without doubt!
Those who do not act according to the command,
Whoever does not abide by the commitments,
Will be burned merely by seeing my form,
And destroyed merely by hearing my sound."
The miracle of this sound arising
Resounds from all the palaces of the Victors.
Use these in keeping with their meaning.

The definitive meaning of what is thus renowned
Should be known by yogins:
All are glorious and bear locks.
Some have auspicious marks,
And likewise there is the paṃcakula.
Some are the naśa of examination,
Likewise unchanging and hard.
The mahākesa is endowed with glory,
And is called endowed with the five wisdoms.
The āyuḥphala is supreme for life.
Some are the mele debyi, stable;
Some likewise the khasvakha,
Endowed with the glory of the khahupa.
Such are those from which auspicious aims arise.
It is complete, endowed with the marks of the Sugata.
The crown knot, the virtuous crown, is endowed with glory,
And so is the jñānamurtti.
Some are the jñānakuṇḍali,
Likewise the wrathful kuṇḍali.
The ekajaṭi is endowed with marks,
And the abimuka is complete.
Stable in desire, it gives rise to bliss;
The meaningful nondual is attained.
The putra increases greatly; the Slender Sapling flourishes.
The vajra lacquered bowl, complete in its marks,
The crystal bowl from which the secret aim arises,
The kapāla of spontaneously present body and speech,
Fulfills all hopes as one wishes.
Likewise one complete in qualities is complete in all.
One that has all the auspicious marks
Is likewise the unchanging, stable svastika,
An indestructible treasury, the source of all.
The sublime one that brings forth signs of miracles appears;
It is supreme for the rite of wholly liberating existence,
Special for bringing the three realms under power,
And supreme for the rite that shakes out the six and the three for good.

The definitive meaning, free of the elaborations of signs:
The six classes, the six colors and the six shapes,
And the qualities and faults, should be matched together.
The secret of many and few:
Increase of the white with its accompaniments, and the head not appearing.
Avoid faults such as imitations.
The word of the Victors that empties the three poisons
Is the svastika sign that ripens the three bodies.
This secret pith instruction on the means of concealment
Is for fortunate yogins.
For all the unfortunate it is the māra of conceptual thought;
How could those who grasp mere names rely on it?
The learned examine and rely on what is good.
As for the great times when accomplishment arises,
One and three are taught as the best;
Likewise five and seven are known as middling;
Nine is the lowest accomplishment,
And from eleven onward it is said to fade away.
Therefore yogins of prowess
Should strive diligently and examine.
Thus spoke the lord of desire.

Because they are signs, they are certain in that;
In order to accord with those attached to signs,
By skillful means act in accord with them,
And steer them toward the true meaning.
To purify them thoroughly,
Drawing in the minds of the six classes of beings, who are greatly distracted,
And having them rely on it,
These are words that join them to the aim of skillful means:
On the ground where the six causes gather,
With the play of song, dance and performance,
Overpowering the three realms of existence and bringing them under power,
One should engage in conduct that accords with the world.
For example, in the world systems,
Beings tame beings:
One enters into the activity they enjoy,
And by acting in harmony with their intentions,
Becomes familiar with them and pleases them.
Then, having won their warmth,
These are the words that change their way of perceiving:
"Before, my friend, my entering into your activity
Was a great mistake.
Count here the accumulation of wrongful deeds.
Though it was wrong, I pleased you.
Now, in this activity that pleases me,
You, my friend, must fully engage."
By doing so, since their former mind has been won,
Their later mind is held as well,
And they enter into the activity that pleases.

The activity of conduct is as follows:
Upon the aggregate in which the six causes gather,
The vajra sign that destroys the abodes of the lower realms,
The melodious song of wisdom and skillful means,
Words proclaiming the marvelous great secret.
On the ground of the five afflictions,
Since the syllables of the five wisdoms shine forth,
Like banks of clouds of blazing blood-drinking herukas,
They play with the steps of the five bulls,
The fierce song of the lion's roar,
Words proclaiming the supreme vajra meaning.
The meaning of marvelous play has come forth:
How wonderful! Marvelous and greatly amazing!
From the blessing of play in delight,
The six streams are cut at the root; greatly wondrous!
From the sky of great equality
The blazing fire of wisdom arose,
And so the ocean of the blood of saṃsāra dried up,
And suffering was brought to exhaustion in the expanse.

From the non-abiding expanse, as a miraculous display,
It blazes in the very essence of fierce and terrifying turmoil.
As clouds of blazing blood-drinkers radiate,
The lake of blood of the three realms of existence is scorched dry.
The steps of the hero, the majestic wrestler;
The wrathful song of the rākṣasa Matraṃ;
The marvelous secret words of the Great Vehicle:
The tethering stake of attachment is pulled out from its depths.
From the blissful expanse of the sphere of the four spaces
Miracles in a hundred thousand clusters radiate.
From the enlightened activity of supremely delighted play,
Some play in wisdom and skillful means;
Some play in the activity of compassion;
Some play in the domain of those to be tamed;
Some play in the expanse of wisdom.
The empowerment of realizing the king of methods,
Through the distinction of the greatness of scripture,
Joins one equally with all the Victors.
The steps of vajra and lotus play;
Words shining in unattached space;
The song that overpowers the three poisons:
In order to purify the world step by step,
Play arose in the world systems.
They play in the heart of the six worlds.
Having overpowered the lord and chief,
The retinue is attended with pleasing words.
Having cut the continuum of the five paths of saṃsāra,
One blends into the state of liberation, space.
The supreme dance moves like lightning.
From the marvelous secret maṇḍala
Miracles of blazing syllables radiate.
From their turbulent radiating and gathering, like stars,
The three realms are reduced, like firewood, to ash.
The steps of the vajra whirlwind and the shooting star;
The song of the great vajra dragon thundering;
The secret words of the vajra that accomplishes the aim:
The armies of the vicious are utterly destroyed.
From the maṇḍala of vajra emanation
Clouds of the mind of awakening, which is skillful means, are sent forth.
Through the special quality of the attachment that magnetizes,
They play with the six maidens of the world.
The steps of the lion's play;
The vajra song of changing calls;
Words that cut attachment at the root:
This is the great meaning of vajra play.
From the maṇḍala of the blazing wheel
Syllables of blazing weapons radiate.
The very essence of the six causes is cut at the root;
The heap of the five poisons is scattered.
The great weapon of the sharp blade of wisdom
Radiates from the expanse where the nature of reality is spread out.
From the force and power of the wealth of supreme skillful means,
Blazing weapons fall like rain.
The rain of syllables, the weapons of wisdom,
Should be made to fall by the force of the vajra tongue.

The enjoyments of the treasure of jewels:
In order to destroy the mountain of conceptual thought
And demolish the heap of the three poisons,
The wheels, the hooks and the rest,
The supports, the great secret, the supreme sesame,
The refined essence and all the other doors,
Having divided them into their respective portions,
As the share allotted to each task
Of purifying afflictions through compassion,
Set out the mudrās, each in its own place.
When it has thus been completed,
Of a size like all the maṇḍal,
In the center, four lines and nine compartments;
Around the perimeter make the eight spokes of a wheel.
On it, four gates together with their gate surrounds;
Draw the mountains of fire of the eon that ends all.
In the center draw the white and the red in union.
In the eight directions and intermediate directions draw eight khaṃ.
On the circular border between the wheel's spokes
Clearly draw eight khaṃ facing inward:
Eight facing inward on the spokes,
And on the eight borders, eight facing outward.
On the four corners, facing inward,
Four kinds of bindu should be drawn.
On the outer courtyards between them,
Twenty-eight bindus
Facing outward should be drawn.
On the fire courtyard of the great eon,
Clearly draw ten bindus facing outward.
On it set out the mudrās in order.
At the gate of the maṇḍal, one great corpse,
Thoroughly purified with song, dance and performance,
Is placed in the center and turned to face the east.
Its aggregates as the celestial palace,
Generated from bhuṃ, should be visualized clearly.
There meditate on the great host of mudrās.
On each of the places of the substances,
Set out the deities of the families in order.
Then perform the vajra approach.
When the signs of the descent of blessing and the indications are satisfied,
Liberate that corpse in the northeast corner.
Having dedicated the substances one by one,
In the places where the mudrās reside,
Set them out and join them again without error.
On the nine glorious vessels
Arrange the nine marvels,
And in each place of great blazing
Meditate on them clearly in a majestic manner.
Set out the other mudrās completely as well.
In the maṇḍala of complete wisdom
Spread out the mighty crescent moons and hosts of stars.
The indestructible vajra blazes ninefold.
Through the distinction of the nine greatnesses,
The nine great glorious ones are complete in the body;
The nine great mothers play in the expanse;
The nine śrīvatsas blaze in the heart;
The nine great elements swirl in the expanse;
The nine cuckoos complete their own calls;
The nine bandha are endowed with glory;
The nine crescent moons are the mother's expanse;
The nine bindus are the mind of awakening;
The nine suns and moons blaze, radiating light.
Thus shining clearly in nines upon nines,
The nine expanses of a single bhaga,
And nine closed, one opened: the meaning of the bindu.
Making all abundance flourish,
Join from nine corpses onward as medicine.
Take the accomplishments of the substances and make use of them.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Sattvavajra.

This is the fourteenth chapter, on the marvelous substances, from the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness.

Then again Sattvavajra spoke thus: "The host of the Great Assembly should know and understand all phenomena directly. What is direct? It is knowing directly, by making mindfulness present, all phenomena that do not abide in objects, all phenomena that will arise later, and all phenomena that arose before. Why is that? Because knowing directly is the cause of great wisdom. And how is that? If one makes mindfulness present of all phenomena as having the nature of an illusion, that is unerring knowledge of the nature of reality made manifest. When the nature of reality unerringly becomes manifest, that is great non-abiding wisdom itself. Therefore, attend to the higher knowledges with mindfulness, directly!"

When he had said this, the great sages of the awareness of higher knowledge (abhijñā) said: "Blessed One, higher knowledge is our commitment. Blessed One, please explain our commitments." To this request, the Blessed One declared: "Great vidyādharas, the higher knowledges, the cause of great wisdom, should be known to be fivefold.

From all the eye faculties
Make a finely ground powder.
Having mixed it into the essence of the elixir,
If one recites the heart mantra of seeing a hundred thousand times,
One will see everything without exception.
This is the higher knowledge of the eye.
The words spoken with the vajra tongue
Should be recited at the proper times or continuously.

vajra cakṣu garbha vidyā hūṃ.
All the ear faculties, too,
Having ground them or made them into powder,
Mix into the essence of the elixir;
If one recites the heart mantra of hearing a hundred thousand times,
One hears all the varieties of sound.
This is the higher knowledge of the ear.
Its examination in vajra words is:
vajra garbha stam si ho.
All the nose faculties, every one,
Having dried them or made them into powder,
Mix into the essence of the elixir;
Having blended them with various fragrances,
If one recites the heart mantra of sensation a hundred thousand times,
One will attain the higher knowledge of the nose.
What is to be held in vajra words is:
vajra na ma si taṃ hūṃ.
All the tongue faculties, every one,
Having dried them or made them into powder,
Mix into the essence of the elixir;
From the supreme of flavors and the rest,
Recite the heart mantra of taste a hundred thousand times.
The maṇḍala of the tongue that speaks:
On a square platform one cubit in size,
In the center draw the secret vajra,
And surround its edge with the lotus of the tongue.
From the tongue rays of light of words radiate.
When this is completed through the practice of speech,
The faculty of speech opens,
And one comes to know all flavors.
This is the higher knowledge of the tongue.
Its examination in vajra words is:
vajra pāśa sityā hūṃ.
All the heart faculties, every one,
Having dried them or made them into powder as well,
Mix with the supreme essence,
And recite the heart mantra of mindfulness a hundred thousand times.
From the maṇḍala of the śrīvatsa, the citta,
Surrounded entirely by the supports of mindfulness,
When the maṇḍala of the heart is complete,
All the faculties become clear,
And one comes to know everything without exception.
This is the supreme knowledge of the minds of others.
vajra citta si dyā hūṃ.
All the body faculties, every one,
Having ground them or made them into powder,
Mix with the ten hard substances;
If one recites the heart mantra of touch a hundred thousand times,
One will attain the higher knowledge of the body.
When these higher knowledges are all complete,
They are the cause of great non-abiding wisdom.
Divination too should be done with the utmost care.

Thus he spoke.

This is the fifteenth chapter, called the method of accomplishing the awareness of higher knowledge, from the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness.

Then Sattvavajra spoke thus: "Host of the Great Assembly, the supreme great method of accomplishment should be understood as follows.

The yogin who strives for the aim
Should make a platform for the rite, broken in half,
Surrounded with lines of fire, and set it blazing.
The maṇḍal of that maṇḍala
Is made in three tiers, its center
Adorned with eight crescent moons.
Its outer edge is ringed with a garland of skulls;
Around that edge it is ringed with blazing masses of fire;
On its outer edge the great red offering is spread.
In the center of the crescent the great glorious one blazes.
On the eight crescents are the eight lords of places,
Dark blue, the yatha, blazing in mountains of fire.
Below that, three rows of skulls,
And the gate courtyards are ringed with substances of the charnel ground.
The tiered gates, the gate surrounds, and lastly the servants:
Arrange everything just as it should be.
When it has become perfectly complete,
Begin the maṇḍal of the four kinds of rites.
In the maṇḍala of the eon of Bhairava,
With the blazing mudrā of the devourer,
In order to liberate the tsara of liberation,
The one who holds the pith instruction and has gained warmth
Should establish the supreme blessing.
Having made the procedure straight through the three purities,
The supreme place is a solitary mountain peak,
Free of eavesdroppers and slanderers,
Where bustle and coming and going have ceased:
That is auspicious as a place for the conduct of the great secret.
Though the signs of the four kinds of rites be few,
If one or two are present, it is said to be middling.
The king of practitioners, endowed with confidence,
Very majestic and steadfast,
Free of hesitation and doubt, holding to the yogic discipline,
His garland of rites without excess or lack,
With fragrant, pure medicines
Washes the outside, and from the śi ne da hu
Makes the inside greatly pure.
Endowed with the command applied as valid authority,
He sets the time and begins in earnest.
And what is of great importance to examine is this:
One who is auspicious and becomes endowed with beauty,
Of full build and in the prime of youth,
Endowed with the marks of the five families,
Suitable as the substance for liberation:
Having sought one who is attractive and prosperous,
That one is made the substance of practice.
One who has such marks and signs
Has trained in the grounds and reached a ground.
As for those supreme causes of practice,
Beyond the hu and below, transform the faculties.
Having brought down the vajra diviner,
One should ask about all of appearance and existence,
Or ask about all that is good and bad.
In the maṇḍala of the five sense doors
Place the bindus of the five kinds of amṛta.
At the five doors from which consciousness arises
Place the substances that arise from each.
Seal each with the mudrā of its own family.
Having set out the mudrās, liberate the afflictions.
Having purified all that is liberated and joined,
Offering, pleasing and fulfilling the commitments,
Take whichever of the three kinds of accomplishment is attained.
Having attained them, use everything as one pleases."

Thus he spoke.

Then Sattvavajra again spoke thus: "Host of the Great Assembly, the great practice that turns back all that obstructs should be understood as follows.

Having begun the rite as above,
Those supreme causes of liberation,
The bones, are accomplished as vajra ash.
The whole mass of bones, every one,
Is burned and mixed with black tongue incense.
Placed inside a Viṣṇu amulet box with its lid joined,
Having accomplished it with the mudrā of destruction
And with wrathful mantras,
When it is accomplished, apply it as the remedy
That turns back the obstacles of the yogin who pursues the aim."

Thus he spoke.

This is the sixteenth chapter, on the sādhana of the great vajra ash, from the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness.

Then again Sattvavajra spoke thus: "Host of the Great Assembly, all the bra should be understood as follows.

On a single square maṇḍal,
With crescent moons and one gate, encircled by vajras,
Draw a border endowed with four gates.
The supreme cause, with half its faculties, is auspicious.
Having performed the outer ablutions,
Place it in the center, facing the east.
Tie a five-colored dhāraṇī cord to the tongue;
The yogins should circle around it.
Lay out the mudrās together with the substances.
Within it generate the celestial palace.
The maṇḍala stacked in tiers:
The five blazing ones, the blazing great elements,
Shine clearly as the five goddesses of meaning.
The ground of all shines clearly as Samantabhadrī.
At the very core of her heart,
On a bindu, a full moon,
The wisdom being, a span in size;
At its heart a precious jewel blazes.
In the center, in an amulet box of the sun,
In a śrīvatsa vase the size of a mustard seed,
A red hūṃ the size of a sesame seed abides:
It is called the mind of awakening of great bliss.
Do not cut the flow of the secret vajra cord.
Perform the vajra mouth divination.
Then, if signs have appeared,
Take the accomplishment and liberate it;
Dry it and make it the root of amṛta.

Thus he spoke.

This is the seventeenth chapter, on the method of accomplishing the lesser supreme through skillful means, from the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness.

Then again Sattvavajra spoke thus: "Host of the Great Assembly, all phenomena should be known to have the nature of an illusion. Why is that? An illusion, though it appears as a thing, is without intrinsic nature, and the meaning of being without intrinsic nature is the vajra of the nature of reality. Therefore all the phenomena of illusion should be understood."

Then the Gaṇapati of the asuras who had gathered in that assembly, called Māyākāra (the Illusionist), said this: "Blessed One, our commitment is to abide in the miraculous display of illusion and to make illusions deliberately. Therefore, for our sake, please explain all the phenomena of illusion."

To this request, the Blessed One declared: "Great Gaṇapati, the miraculous display of illusion should be understood as follows.

All phenomena appear as illusions;
The nature of illusion is emptiness;
And whatever the meanings of emptiness are,
They are the meaning of the unchanging, indestructible vajra.
Within emptiness many millions
Of miraculous displays of illusion arise.
Likewise, the miraculous displays of illusion too,
From mere trifling causes and conditions,
Appear as distinct miraculous forms.
Though they appear, they are not established as things;
Being without change or destruction, they are the meaning of vajra.
The characteristic of conventional illusion, too, is this:
From causes such as pebbles and sticks,
Conditions are joined by means of medicines and mantras,
And the result, the miraculous display of illusion, arises.
As for applying the secret words of illusion,
The lord of illusion explained it thus:
Make the desired form from the cause,
Apply the śeu reu medicine to it,
And perform the application of this secret mantra:
śeu reu i na ya
a mo kha a gussa gus.
If one strikes it with switches,
One may produce various things just as one wishes.
If one wishes to gather it back, reverse it.
Therefore those distracted by illusion
Should be guided by means that draw them in."

This is the eighteenth chapter, on the method of accomplishing the illusion vajra, from the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness.

Then again Sattvavajra spoke thus: "Host of the Great Assembly, if you wish to have power over all phenomena, understand it as follows.

Being endowed with every excellence,
On a platform square on all sides,
Encircled by a single border,
Join it in the shape of a full moon disk.
Provide it with four gates and four corners.
On the bindu, round in shape,
Encircle it with vajras and suns.
In the center, on a glorious bandha,
Place the Great Glorious One, father and mother in union.
On the eight segments set the eight places,
Circling the hub, placed in order.
At the eastern gate of the bindu,
Write the secret name of the vajra yogin
And the accomplishment to be requested, and place it there.
In what has become the offering courtyard,
Lay out the substances of practice and the offerings
Beautifully, in their proper order.
Those vajra yogins
Are marked with the sign of the vajra's descent.
Having completed the maṇḍala of samādhi,
Invite the deities and gather all the offerings;
Having completed the entry, the request for accomplishments,
And all the rites of empowerment,
The marvelous tormas
Are divided in three portions: the first is made into a fire offering;
The middle one is hidden as an inexhaustible treasure:
In the river of the mind of awakening,
In the great expanse of the space of the nature of reality,
And in the ground of the bhaga of the mother,
Place it in a manner that is well hidden.
For one portion, from the eastern gate of the palace,
Make a platform at a distance of thirteen measures.
There the chiefs of the seven places too,
Likewise once for each of the thirteen,
And the mothers and sisters, guests of the places,
Should all be completed, up to seventy.
Lastly, on the crescent maṇḍal,
If one is able, the sky offering of the ḍākinīs:
Complete it without remainder and please them.
Complete all that remains.
All that has become dregs
Hide, or burn, or cast into a river.
Guard that great vajra place;
In order to hide the place, keep it secret from all.
Since it is not fitting, the signs
Should go each to its own place.
Whenever they gather, it is likewise.
This is the method of accomplishing the single bindu,
So the Victor taught.
The person who accomplishes these,
Abiding on the wheel of the moon,
And holding aloft the wheel of the sun,
Becomes a king who has power
Over the true expanse of phenomena itself."

Thus he spoke.

Again Sattvavajra spoke thus:
"Yogins of the Great Assembly,
This too, step by step as above,
Having arranged everything properly,
On the outside, square on all sides,
Make a single moon bindu.
By crossing four threads upon it,
Make it into nine compartments.
Encircle the outer border with skulls,
And adorn the square and its corners with great beauty.
On the single courtyard at its outer edge,
The face of the gate looks toward the practitioner.
In the center, on the white and red bindus
In union, joined between by a vajra,
There the Great Glorious One, father and mother, resides.
On those eight outer compartments
Make eight outstretched crescent moons,
And set out the eight mothers of the places of wisdom.
On that outer courtyard with its single gate
Lay out the substances of practice beautifully.
What is to be visualized, the practice and all the rest,
Should all be applied here in the same way.
Whatever person accomplishes it in this way
Becomes a king of the Dharma;
He becomes a great being
Who has power over all things."

Thus he spoke.

Then again Sattvavajra spoke this, called "Mastery over All Miracles":

"Yogin of the Great Assembly,
Apply this too entirely in the same way.
Making it square on every side,
Make a wheel of eight spokes.
Outside its four corner sections
Furnish it with four gates.
The outer courtyard, an expanse of dark red mountains of fire,
Draw exceedingly majestic and terrifying.
On the heart at the center of the bindu
Mark it with a blazing crescent moon.
On the eight supreme spokes draw
Those eight crescent moons facing inward.
On the eight spaces between the spokes, the same;
And the four vajra gatekeeper goddesses.
Having laid out the mudrās in their order,
Complete all the activities of practice.
The yogin who accomplishes it thus
Becomes a king who holds mastery
Over all miracles, both worldly
And beyond the world."

Thus he spoke.

From the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness, this is the nineteenth chapter, called "Accomplishing the Bindu."

Then Sattvavajra spoke these words: "Yogins of the Great Assembly, if you wish to have mastery over all the hosts of the haughty ones, you should understand it as follows:

The activities of what is to be done and so forth,
With whatever wealth and excellence there is,
Skill in means and the implements,
Should be gathered here as one understands them.
Then, on something like a maṇḍala plate,
Make the charnel ground with a head as well.
At the four corners make four maṇḍalas,
And from the four, those four are praised in turn.
Make that same one in the form of a mātaṅga,
Crossed at the center from the heart downward,
And place it beneath the great maṇḍala.
Lay down the lines of a blazing wheel
And make the mudrās of the five Great Glorious Ones.
On that which is beautified by two courtyards
Set out the mudrās of the sacred places and lands.
At the four gates, the four vajra gatekeepers;
There stand the effigies of the emanated limbs.
Make the outer courtyard a vajra mountain of fire;
Outside it, fill it with great garlands,
And encircle it with ramparts, dry and moist.
Outside that, sparks of fire swirl;
Draw the whirlwind at the end of the aeon.
At the vajra gate-arches and gatehouses
Draw coils of guggulu and latticework,
Corpses and bundles of snakes.
Then, on a glorious skull bowl,
Write the letters of Vajraśrī,
Join it mouth to mouth, and set it face down.
On small amulet boxes draw
Eight matted-haired ones and place them in the place of the elements.
For eight or twelve agents,
Draw twelve agents and arrange them.
In the maṇḍala of the place of liberation,
The outcastes, the haughty bhūtas,
The cause of corruption by attachment and aversion:
In order to destroy their three poisons,
The great accomplishment of the meaning is taught.
The great host of the five Rudras,
Vanquished by the five wisdoms of samādhi,
Are thoroughly joined with vajra heat,
On the ground of the sūtra, at a single stroke.
The obstructors of obstructors, the great obstructors,
Are born from the five poisons, from conceptuality itself.
In the two and a half charnel grounds,
The base and root are speech, and body and mind.
Majestic Great Glorious One, lord of the charnel ground,
Through the samādhi that crushes self-conception and conception,
In the charnel ground of Rudra of the three realms of existence,
Make those eight great Gaṇapatis.
Opening the door of the dark house of ignorance,
Seizing that mad elephant,
Cut the tether rope of rigid apprehended and apprehender,
Pull out the tethering stake of the three poisons and conceptual thought.
Having drawn them out of the mire of the lower realms,
Having subdued that wild army of afflictions,
Thoroughly purified by wisdom and means,
In the expanse of space, without reference point,
The great hosts of offerings of great bliss,
Joined evenly with the five supreme tastes,
Spread out the enjoyment of the Dharma.
Through the compassion of equality and bliss,
To crush the great haughty ones
And to cut the net of saṃsāra,
Clouds of blazing wrathful hosts radiate,
Hosts fierce, short, terrifying and swift.
Having let fall a great rain of emissaries,
Having cast off the dust of saṃsāra,
Plowing the field of great bliss,
Making the sprout of awakening grow,
Raise the harvest of bodhisattvas.
As for the facing of the great charnel ground,
Its head is laid toward the southwest,
Its feet are laid toward the northeast.
I am the great Īśvara of the charnel ground,
The body of the buddhas, which is wisdom.
Against the army, with sharp compassion,
With the weapon of sharp wisdom,
I cut the stream of saṃsāra of wrong conception.
Having built a hearth, square with four sides,
Shaped like a vajra,
Make it the hearth of the wheel at the heart.
Stack up the kindling of the five families,
Build the great activity joined with the five homas.
In the homa pit of the five great means,
On the kindling of the suffering of saṃsāra,
In the fiery expanse of great wisdom,
Complete the burning and purifying of the five afflictions.
All the applications of the activities without exception
Should be done as resources allow and as is fitting."

Thus he spoke.

From the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness, this is the twentieth chapter, called "Suppressing the Haughty Ones."

Then again Sattvavajra spoke these words: "Host of the Great Assembly, the meaning of the practice called the Maṇḍala of the Mouth That Exhausts the Ocean of Saṃsāra should be understood as follows:

"Having applied this too entirely in the same way,
In a celestial palace with surrounding chambers,
Of the measure of one cubit, square,
The four are those surrounding chambers and courtyards:
Construct them in the four directions.
Strew sweet-smelling sandalwood and paste;
Fragrances, incense, heaps of jewels:
Set out these gifts and offerings in abundance.
The four kinds of divine lay devotees
Command to the tasks of guarding the directions and intermediate directions.
In the inner tier of the courtyard, in the directions and intermediate directions,
Ten half-sized platforms;
Draw the blazing mass of fire at the end of the aeon,
And, empowering ten suitable images
Made of various materials,
Seal the inner boundary with the dance of the wrathful ones.
Then Matraṃ is to be suppressed.
This haughty, sinful Rudra
Is ever a fit object for liberation.
jaḥ hūṃ baṃ hoḥ mahā śrī heruka matraṃ rudra ha ha phra be śa phaṭ
Having toppled him with the mudrā of the khaṭvāṅga,
Having opened the door of the darkness of ignorance,
Blessing his consciousness as the letter A,
Dissolve it into the expanse of the mind of the great secret.
Make the interior a blazing palace.
On the platform resembling the form of Matraṃ,
Set it toward whatever direction suits the manner of his toppling.
Its color blue-grey, in the manner of lying on his back,
Its size without fixed measure, adapted to the place.
At its heart, three tiers:
First build a red square,
Second make a dark blue triangle,
Third make a dark maroon crescent.
On the four limbs, the head and the genitals, six,
And the two secret openings, eight in all,
Eight half-sized square platforms.
On its outermost tier, all around,
Make a maṇḍala of clear crescent moons,
And surround them with blazing expanses of fire.
On top, upon the crescent moons,
The glorious supreme deity resides.
On the dark blue triangle draw a mass of fire.
On the red square tier
Place the glorious supreme deities of the four families.
On the eight, right and left, of the limbs, head and genitals,
The eight mistresses of the places of Īśvara:
Draw them, or else set down their symbols.
On the eight great joints, the eight lords of the lands:
Do as is fitting.
At the four gates set the secret meditation enclosures.
On the crescent moon of the outermost rim
Place twenty-eight women,
Placing them by name or with a symbol as support.
At its mouth build a hearth,
A hearth shaped like a lotus, a śrīvatsa or the expanse.
The skilled should prepare the kindling.
All the tasks that are to be done,
Apply just as they were applied above."

Thus he spoke.

From the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness, this is the twenty-first chapter, called "The Practice That Liberates All Saṃsāra."

Then again Sattvavajra spoke this sūtra called "Mastering the Three Poisons as Enlightened Mind":

"Yogin of the Great Assembly,
The mastering of the three poisons, too,
Should be understood in this way.
Whatever is the nature of the three poisons
Is the very essence of the three bodies;
There is nothing at all to seek elsewhere.
When it has come to be understood thus,
In the conventional way transform it thus.
Applying everything as above,
On a platform one fathom in size,
Make the outside red and broad.
Then build a dark blue triangle,
Then make a dark red crescent.
In the center of the crescent the Great Glorious One blazes;
The eight places of Īśvara on the crescent's rim;
Between the tiers the charnel grounds blaze.
Last, on the red square,
Make four gates with their gatekeepers.
Surround its outer edge with mountains of fire,
And set out the emblems of the ten blazing wrathful ones.
The practice, the entry and the empowerments
Should be bestowed as above.
By this everything is complete,
And all three poisons are overwhelmed."

Thus he spoke.

From the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness, this is the twenty-second chapter, the chapter of the practice called "Mastering the Three Poisons."

Then the sublime Sattvavajra spoke this great maṇḍala called "Mastery over All Women": "Host of the Great Assembly, if you wish to bring all who are attached under your power, you should understand it as follows:

Woman is the nature of attachment;
Attachment is the ocean of blood.
Sentient beings in saṃsāra, through their karma,
Sink in the ocean of the blood of attachment.
Liberation from attachment is cessation;
That very attachment is called blood.
In order to dry up the ocean of blood,
The oceans of women's blood
Are transformed into the ocean of the nectar of life.
When it has come to be understood thus,
In the conventional way apply it thus:
Having undertaken, in proper order,
The perfect undertaking of the activity,
On a square maṇḍala one fathom in size
Lay down the lines of four wheels.
On the outer tier of the rim, four corner sections;
Then make the gate courtyards red.
The outermost tier of all is sealed off
By blazing mountains of the fire of lakchuhwa.
In the center draw a crescent facing east,
And set out the glorious skull bowl, father and mother.
The four wheels hold the four families.
On the two intermediate courtyards, the sacred places and lands,
Make them furnished with crescent and bindu.
The gate courtyards: four gates with crescents.
The outer courtyard: the fire of lakchuhwa,
And the wrathful ones of fire, with bindus.
Adorn it all with the ornaments of the charnel ground.
The completion of samādhi, the actual practice and faith,
The entry, the empowerment, the accomplishments,
Guarding the place and the rest: complete them all."

Thus he spoke.

From the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness, this is the twenty-third chapter, on the method of practice of mastery through the wheel.

Then the sublime Sattvavajra declared these words: "Host of the Great Assembly, all phenomena should be known to have the nature of nectar medicine. Why is that? If one realizes the meaning of all these phenomena that appear in this way, it is life, the elixir of the nature of reality. If one does not realize it, it is death, the poison of saṃsāra. Therefore all these phenomena should be known to have the nature of nectar. Having known this, one should practice conventionally as follows:

Having arranged it comfortably, with whatever is available,
At the heart of Matraṃ's torso,
In keeping with the measure of one fathom,
Lay out a blazing wheel of eight spokes,
And surround it with the light of fire.
Its edge, encircled by a vajra fence,
Make with blazing gate-arches.
In the center make an eight-faceted jewel,
And there set out the blazing mudrā.
In this supreme place where Laṅkā was liberated,
The hosts of the matted-haired ones gather;
All the great powers that crush the Rakṣa
Liberate the whole of the three realms of existence here.

"With the sacred substances offered in the four seasons,
Make offerings to them as is fitting.
With the essence of the ripened nourishment of the four seasons,
Make the substances of the great accomplishment.
The great substances of the four directions and the center:
The wondrous matted-haired ones
Hold mastery over the five elements
And accomplish the miracles of the five elements:
Summer water, autumn fire, winter earth,
Spring wind, and space, joined in secret.
Having gained mastery over those miracles,
Practice them as the matted-haired ones.
Through the clarity of the five kinds of samādhi,
The five kinds of meat that arise from the five elements,
Dog meat, ox, horse meat,
And various birds and wild animals,
Are the substances for swiftly accomplishing miraculous powers.
The great substances of practice, the five great elements,
Give mastery over all the world.
When the substances are thus to hand,
Having meditated in samādhi and practiced,
And applied everything in keeping with the above,
The entry, the accomplishments and the great empowerments
Apply with ease, as is fitting."

Thus he spoke. This is the explanation of the sūtra called "The Self-Arisen Maṇḍala of Nectar."

Then again Sattvavajra spoke these words:

"Yogin of the Great Assembly,
Furthermore, for the great nectar,
Having properly applied everything as above,
On a maṇḍala of the fitting size,
A red lotus, a sky of light;
On the outer tier, four red crescents,
Beautifully encircled by blazing vajras.
A garland of blazing tongues of flame
Should be drawn all around the crescents.
With a garland of jewel anthers
Encircle the petals of the lotus.
Make the four gates of blazing jewels.
When the deities of the blazing mudrās are arranged:
In the center, in a smooth golden bowl,
Set a great conch with a spiral.
In the east, in a crystal bowl with upright fluting,
Set a clear jewel ūrṇā.
In the south, in a jeweled bowl with many flutings,
Set lush jewel leaves.
In the west, in a porcelain bowl shaped like a crescent,
Set two jewel swellings at the space between the brows.
In the north, in a lacquer bowl shaped like a cross,
An uṣṇīṣa swelling with four jewel knobs.
Apply the five roots to their respective directions,
And the chiefs of the five gates according to family.
From the center, then east, south, west and north,
Kakkola and turuṣka,
Jātiphala, fragrant kha,
And white chebok: apply them in this order.
Likewise, in the same order as above,
Myrobalan and camphor,
Saffron, lotus and ligadur:
These are the rasāyanas.
All of them should be applied in keeping
With family, color and activity.
Apply the five seeds in order.
Inside the glorious vessel
Place the essences of father and mother in sequence,
Setting them in the center and on the lotus petals.
Set out the sacred places, lands and the rest beautifully.
Arrange the charnel-ground substances and auspicious substances,
And all the implements that are to hand.
Then bring down the blessing and request the accomplishments;
Everything should be thoroughly applied.
That very sublime one, of the great secret,
Through the hundred families, the five families and the one family,
Should be made the supreme purifier of all.
Then, when the ingredients are purified,
Clearing, dedicating, gathering and separating,
Bathing and confession,
Proclaiming the qualities and bringing down the blessing are to be done.
Then join them for an instant, a moment,
Or a single day.
If signs appear, it is mastered there.
As for preparing the supreme powder:
The medicines in the vessel are gathered by the wisdom consort;
In the rite of grinding them too,
The mother holds and the father grinds.
Bring down and invite the skillful vidyādharas,
And recite the mantras of each activity.
The grinding stone and the pestle are means and wisdom;
Prepare the medicine itself as the mind of awakening.
The goddess takes up this vajra song:
'In the palace of Samantabhadrī,
From the union of means and wisdom,
Saṃsāra is prepared as nectar medicine.
From its clarity as the five wisdoms,
The fruit, the three bodies, is perfected as enlightened mind.
With the deathless nectar, chronic illness is cured.'
To examine the signs, having weighed it,
Pour it into the glorious vajra vessels joined mouth to mouth.
Wrap it in cloth of the colors of the five families,
Seal it with the seal of vajra power,
And bind it with the dhāraṇī of the vajra mantra.
Enthrone it as the five bodies of the Great Glorious One,
And confer the empowerment of the five wisdoms.
It is to be set upon the maṇḍala.
All circle around it and bring down the accomplishments.
The lords of elixirs and medicines,
And the subtle vidyādharas bearing signs,
Attend upon it with many instruments and costumes.
Fill the practice with the finest grain;
Cover it lavishly with auspicious cloth;
Offer knotted dhāraṇī cords according to family.
Purify it thoroughly with the fire of samādhi.
Whichever of the five great signs appears,
Be quick to take the accomplishments.
Having drawn the nectar forth from sound,
In the glorious one endowed with the lotus,
All is nectar, the samaya.
In the vast expanse of space great bliss is perfected.
One's own self is the perfect maṇḍala of nectar;
There is nothing whatever to accomplish elsewhere.
The entry and all the rest, too,
Should be done extensively, in the proper way."

Thus he spoke. This is the explanation of the practice of nectar powder.

Then again Sattvavajra spoke these words:

"Yogin of the Great Assembly,
Having applied everything in the same way,
On the maṇḍala, a wheel of eight spokes,
Having completed the deities of the mudrās,
And having purified, ground, sifted and weighed the medicines,
Make pellets with mead and milk.
Place them inside a lacquer bowl bearing the marks.
Join it well mouth to mouth and wrap it in cloth.
Having applied everything as above,
Make the supreme accomplishments your own."

Thus he spoke. This is the explanation of the rite of nectar pills.

Then again Sattvavajra spoke these words:

"Yogin of the Great Assembly,
The rite of the nectar paste is as follows.
Having applied everything as above,
When clear signs appear,
Take the powder out from within the water strainer.
That which has become the finest of powders,
Those queens of powders,
Pour into lacquer bowls joined mouth to mouth.
Having sealed, blessed and empowered them,
Recite, with emanating and gathering, without interruption.
Mead, rice beer, molasses beer,
Grape wine, milk and the like,
All the nourishing juices, mix them in.
Pour it into a jeweled amulet box bearing the marks,
Join it mouth to mouth and seal it,
And, having done the emanating and gathering and the recitation as above,
When signs appear,
Take the great accomplishment.
Those substances of the powder practice
Divide into five portions;
From the portions of the individual yogins,
One portion, as much as suffices,
And from the mixing beer divided into five portions,
From the portions of the individual yogins,
Mix them and make them the support of the accomplishments.
By eating it for one month
The body hair grows full and the wrinkles smooth out.
If it is eaten for two months
All one's splendor blazes forth.
By the benefit of three months
One is freed from aeons of illness.
From four months on, a person who,
Not mixing it with food, practices diligently,
Is able to destroy the illnesses of others.
In the sixth, the seventh and the eighth,
One lives on the essence of nectar.
In the ninth and the tenth, the outflows are exhausted.
In the eleventh, the vidyādhara flies into the sky."

Thus he spoke. This is the explanation of the practice of nectar paste.

Then again Sattvavajra spoke these words:

"Yogin of the Great Assembly,
The food of the vajra vidyādharas of the five skills,
The great rite of the nectar dough,
Should be understood in this way.
Honey, molasses and grapes
Having been prepared as a year's sustenance,
A red cow of fine color, bearing the marks,
Is to be taken and kept as a year's sustenance.
The milk of that cow, fed continually
On the king of medicines, or something close to it,
Becomes the supreme food of vidyādharas.
The queen of nectars, or something close to it,
Having been made into the finest powder
And prepared as a year's sustenance,
In a place where no one will see,
Having spread out those substances of practice,
Practice the nectar in the same way.
At the proper times as the seasons come,
All of those substances of practice
Make into a dough with cow's milk;
Increase it portion by portion and eat it.
The qualities of eating it are inconceivable:
With the outflows exhausted, one goes into the sky."

Thus he spoke. This is the explanation of the practice of nectar dough.

Then again Sattvavajra spoke these words:

"Yogin of the Great Assembly,
Doing this too as above,
The various substances of practice, unlike those,
All the medicines that turn into nectar,
Every single one, should be gathered.
The finest of rendered oils, great sesame and the rest,
Having gathered them all and mixed them,
And having sought out a place as above,
Spread out the nectar and practice.
Do not break the stream of emanating, gathering and recitation.
When it has become an accomplished substance,
The medicines should be ground into powder.
When they have become powder,
They should be mixed with the great rendered oils.
When the rite of preparation is complete,
The supreme accomplishment should be taken.
With others, or those of other factions,
Keep apart for a year and do not meet.
Live on nectar mixed with rendered oil.
The great signs that the nectar is accomplished:
The hair stands on end and one's splendor blazes;
Free of coarse food, one lives in bliss;
Wisdom is clear and unobstructed;
With the outflows exhausted, one goes into the sky."

Thus he spoke. This is the explanation of the practice of the great rendered essence of nectar.

Then again Sattvavajra spoke these words:

"Yogin of the Great Assembly,
If you practice medicine with moist substances,
Having applied everything as above,
On a maṇḍala of five lines,
Having set down the five glorious auspicious ones,
The five chief ones and the five fruits,
Arranged while moist, purify them with the syllables.
By kindling the fire of samādhi and recitation,
First the juices come out and they are dried;
Then grind and sift them as above,
Mix them, and purify them by the approach of the family.
When signs appear from this,
Likewise use them at ease, as above."

Thus he spoke. This is the explanation of the practice of the moist preparation of nectar.

From the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness, this is the twenty-fourth chapter, the chapter on the practice of nectar medicine.

Then the great Sattvavajra explained this, called "The Practice of the Mind of the Great Secret, the Essence of All Practices":

"Yogins of the Great Assembly, everything should be understood in terms of the expanse. All the phenomena of saṃsāra are gathered in the expanse, and all the phenomena of nirvāṇa are gathered in wisdom. Know that the nonduality of these two is gathered in the great bliss of the nature of reality, the mind of awakening. Therefore all phenomena are gathered into the nature of the wisdom mind, and are nondual. That being so, conventionally one should understand it as follows, and so enter into the meaning of nonduality.

Yogin of the Great Assembly,
All the phenomena of the ultimate,
Every one, are complete in one's own mind.
Whatever conventional phenomena there are
Are taught to be complete in body and speech.
Therefore all these phenomena
Are complete in one's own root.
And the supreme method for practicing them:
Having cleared away the dust of conceptual thought,
The two kings of practice, father and mother,
Having become skilled in the meaning,
On the heart of each, bindus
On white and red crescents should be placed.
Its great and supreme substances,
The great camphor of means
And the secret sandalwood of wisdom, the two,
Should be made into great white and red crescents.
On the white one at the father's heart,
The mouth of the bindu faces downward.
On the red one at the mother's heart,
The mouth of the bindu faces upward.

In the center of the father's bindu,
Within a śrīvatsa, draw a sun;
At its center a clear oṃ, and within it hūṃ.
In the center of the mother's bindu,
Within a lotus, draw a moon;
At its center a clear a, and within it hūṃ.
When it has become clear in this way,
As Samantabhadra, father and mother,
Transform it into nonduality.
When the samādhi has become clear,
With the nectar that arises from oneself
Make the offering of the two secrets.
The accomplishments that arise from oneself
Should be dissolved into oneself.
If one makes this practice the principal one,
All the requisites of nectar
And whatever has become the finest of essences
Having been prepared as a year's sustenance,
Of the four branches of approach and accomplishment,
At the time of the approach
The person relies on human food.
That, too, is applied for one year's span,
Or for up to two or three.
For the span of a year
One relies on the requisites of nectar.
Then, for a year's span,
For one or for two,
With the five kinds of substances that arise from oneself
One offers to the noble ones and eats them oneself.
By this one comes to abide in warmth.
From the time warmth is attained,
In a year's span, in a moment,
Having found the taste of the food of samādhi,
One becomes free of coarse food.
Having perfected the three kinds of great warmth,
One attains the great signs.
The miraculous signs that appear are these:
If one wishes for heaps of jewels,
They are accomplished from earth, stones and the like.
If one wishes to travel on water or in the sky,
By setting out, one does not sink and goes unhindered.
If one wishes to quell a mass of fire,
By raising the threatening forefinger
And giving a command of truth, it is accomplished.
One is able to do all these and more.
From the attainment of the signs onward, one is a buddha.

Thus he spoke. This is the explanation of the practice of the mind of the great secret.

From the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Tantra of the All-Gathering Awareness, this is the twenty-fifth chapter, the chapter on the practice of the wheel of the secret word.

Then the Blessed One Sattvavajra entered into the samādhi called "The Blazing Vajra Expanse of Fire." Then all the hosts of the assembly saw all phenomena appear as having the nature of fire. Then the retinue offered high praise:

"How wonderful! Great being, great one,
Great samādhi, great radiance,
Great miracle, great variety,
Supreme means, homage to you!
All phenomena appear as an expanse of fire;
Fire itself is the expanse of clear wisdom.
Since it has blazed, radiated and become clear,
Darkness is dispelled and all things are burned.
That all is perfected in the expanse of great bliss
Is your deed, O hero.
Great compassion, a la la!
Great accomplishment of aims, a la la!
Great grace, a la la!
Great Bhairava, a la la!
Great delight, a la la!
Great haughty one, a la la!
O hero, by your blessing,
Give us an explanation.
The great nature of fire,
Please explain it to us."

When they had made this request, Sattvavajra was pleased and declared these words:

"Yogins of the Great Assembly,
I shall explain the nature of vajra fire;
Listen, all of you, with respect.
Through this supreme activity of vajra fire,
By applying it with ease as the method of yoga,
All the victors are pleased, and all is fulfilled by burning;
It is taught as the supreme activity that accomplishes aims.
In the homa pit of unborn purity,
On the kindling of the birth of phenomena,
With the fire of unelaborated wisdom of awakening,
The habitual tendencies of signs are burned, purified and dissolved.
In the homa pit of the pure bhaga,
On the heaped kindling of desire,
Kindling the fire of the awakening of great bliss,
The things of saṃsāra are burned, purified and dissolved.
In the homa pit of the Great Glorious One's mouth,
On the kindling of the five afflictions,
Kindling the fire of the five wisdoms,
The substances of apprehended and apprehender are burned, purified and dissolved.
In the homa pit of the sky of appearance and existence,
On the kindling of the four elements,
Kindling the fire of the host of mudrās,
The things of appearance and existence are burned, purified and dissolved.
According to the particulars of each of the four activities,
Apply direction, color, shape and substance
Just as they are.
East, south, west and north, apply white, yellow, red,
Dark blue-black and ash color.
Round, with lotus petals, for pacifying;
A wheel, square, with eight corners, for enriching;
A crescent, a segment, the shape of a bow, for magnetizing;
A triangle, with the mouth of a bhaga, for wrathful action.
It is beautiful with three tiers, with luster and designs.
The kindling of the activity is enriched with substances.
Endowed with the activity lineage of the wisdom vajra,
The supreme method is applied by the three masters,
Holding the limit of purity, holding the supreme.
With song, music and dance of the vajra secret,
The secret offerings are joined to the times,
And the aspiration of the vajra path is applied.
Those endowed with the secret activity lineage,
By setting out the deity lineage of pacifying,
And setting out the lineages of enriching and magnetizing,
Should be furnished with deity, mantra and mudrā,
